129and147,whichwerecategorizedinto24compliancedeterminations.
Ofthe24compliancedeterminations,17metorexceededthe95percentcompliancestandard
establishedbytheMonitor,andsevendidnot.Thesevendidnotmeetthestandardswithrespect
to1)immediatenotificationoftheCUOF;2)separationofofficers;3)seven-dayreviewof

performanceevaluations;5)returnstothefieldafterpsychologicalevaluation;6)documentation
ofconsiderationofofficerworkhistoryfordisciplinaryaction;and,7)timelysubmissiontothe
PoliceCommission.

whichthecomplianceincreasedandfourareasinwhichthecompliancedecreased.Therewere

TheComplaint,Form1.28,InvestigationsAudit,PhaseII,wasconductedpursuanttoConsent
Decree(CD)Paragraphs(¶)74(h),75,77,78,80(a-g),81,82,84,85,86,90,91,101,and
129(b,c,andd),whichwerecategorizedinto16objectives.
TheDepartmentmetthestandardsfor14ofthe16objectives.TheDepartmentwas91percent
compliantforeachoftheremainingtwoobjectivesasdetailedbelow.
ObjectiveNo.5(f)–CollectionsandPreservationofEvidenceinProfessionalStandards
Bureau(PSB)Investigations
TheDepartmentdidnotmeetthestandardsforthisobjectiveasitwasunabletoretrievetape
recordingsassociatedwithfourPSBinvestigations.Therewere232tapesidentifiedinthe46
investigations.Ofthe232tapesthatwererequested,227wereobtainedandreviewed.Thefive
tapesthatwerenotobtainedrepresentedtwopercentofthetotalnumberrequested.Although
thisdidnotappeartobeasystemicissue,theauditrecommendedthattheproperstorageand
preservationoftapesassociatedwithcomplaintinvestigationsshouldcontinuetobethefocusof
commandingofficersthroughregularaudits.
ObjectiveNo.16–AdequacyoftheInvestigation
Sevenofthe80investigationsdidnotmeetthestandardsforthisobjective.Theseven
investigationswereconductedbyPSBpersonnelandprimarilydidnotmeetthestandards
becausewitnesseswhoseinformationcouldhaveaddedsignificantlytotheinvestigationwere
notinterviewed.

TheGangEnforcementDetailSelectionCriteriaAuditwasconductedpursuanttoConsent
Decree(CD)Paragraphs(¶)51(bandd),106(b,c,andd),107(a,b,andc),and131(b),which
werecategorizedintosixobjectives.
Theaudittestedofficers’andsergeants’selectionsintoGangEnforcementDetails(GED),
CommunityLawEnforcementandRecoveryunits(CLEAR),andOperations-SouthBureau
(OSB)gangdetail,aswellastheirsuitabilityforthepositions,andwhetherextensionsfor
limitedtourassignmentscompliedwithCDmandatesandDepartmentpolicy.
TheDepartmentmetthestandardsfortwoofthesixobjectives.Adeterminationofcompliance
waswithheldforoneobjective,astherewasinsufficientdatatosupportafinding.Thethree
non-compliantobjectivesaredetailedbelow.
ObjectiveNo1(b)–EligibilityCriteriaforSelectionofSupervisors
Thereductionofthecompliancepercentagefromtheprioryearaudit(100percent)tothecurrent
yearaudit(91percent)wasduetoonesupervisornotbeing“wheeled”asrequiredbytheCDand
Departmentpolicy.Therewere11supervisorypackagestestedforthisobjective.Thesingle
findingcausedonepackagetobeheldoutofcompliance,representingninepercentofthe
sample.
ObjectiveNo.1(c)–PositiveEvaluationofanOfficerbasedupononOfficer’sTEAMS
RecordandWrittenConsiderationofSustainedComplaints,AdverseJudicialFindings,or
Discipline
Thereductionofthecompliancepercentagefromtheprioryearaudit(100percent)tothecurrent
yearaudit(94percent)wasduetofourpackagesnotmeetingalldocumentaryrequirementsfor
GEDselection.Eachofthefourpackageswasforanofficerwhoinitiallywasloanedtothe
GEDandsubsequentlywaspermanentlyselected.
ObjectiveNo1(d)–EvaluationoftheSelectionProcess
TheDepartmentcontinuestobeoutofcomplianceregardingthisobjective.Themostrecurrent
findingwasthatthetwomostrecentPerformanceEvaluationReports(PERs)duewerenot
includedinthepackages.Afeasibilityanalysisispendingregardingtheprioryear’saudit
recommendationthatthe“twomostrecent”PERsbedefinedasthosedueimmediatelypriorto
thestartoftheselectionprocess.

EXECUTIVESUMMARY
NON-CATEGORICALUSEOFFORCEREPORTSAUDIT
Conductedby
AuditDivision
FourthQuarter,FiscalYear2006/07
TheNon-CategoricalUseofForceReports(NCUOF)AuditwasconductedpursuanttoConsent
Decree(CD)Paragraphs(¶)65,68,69,80(c,e,andf),82,128,129,and131(a,c,ande).
TheDepartmentdidnotmeetthestandardsforcomplianceinsixofthe20objectivesthatwere
categorizedintothreeareasasfollows.
SupervisoryandManagementReviewoftheInvestigation
Deficiencieswerenotedinthewatchcommanderassessmentsofofficeractions,including
“canned”language,inappropriate“cutandpaste,”andinsufficientinsightintotheincident.
Further,investigatingsupervisorsapprovedrelatedreports,andsupervisorsinvolvedinor
witnessingtheuseofforceconductedinvestigations,contrarytoDepartmentpolicy.
AdequacyoftheInvestigation
Deficiencieswerealsonotedregardinginterviewsofat-scenesupervisors,photographingof
suspectorofficerinjuries,andthearticulationofthescopeofcanvassingthesceneforwitnesses,
asrequired.
TimelyCompletionofNCUOFInvestigations
InDecember2005,theDepartmentconvertedtoprocessingNCUOFinvestigationsonthe
automatedsystem(TEAMSII).AlthoughTEAMSIIhasmadesignificantimprovementsonthe
technicalaspectsofexpeditinganautomatedNCUOFinvestigation,investigativeand
administrativereviewdelayscontinuetoplaguetheprocess.DuringSeptember2006,128
NCUOFinvestigationswereinitiated.AsofApril30,2007(sevenmonthslater),only25(20%)
hadbeencompleted(i.e.,reviewedandclosedbytheCommandingOfficer,UOFRD).The
minimumaveragenumberofdaystocloseaNCUOFinvestigationfromtheArea/divisionto
acceptancebytheCommandingOfficer,UseofForceReviewDivisionwas166days.Because
therewerestill103investigationsnotcompleted,itisexpectedthattheaveragenumberofdays
tocloseallinvestigationsinitiatedinSeptemberwillbesignificantlyhigher.
Thepreviousauditdeterminedthatthemostpressingareaofnon-compliancewasthe
Department’sfailuretoensuretheaccuraterepresentationoftapedandwrittenstatementsby
investigators.TheDepartmenthassignificantlyimprovedinthisarea,andisnowcompliant.
OtherimprovementsincompliancerelatedtoconsistencyofinformationinNCUOFreportsand
theevaluationofon-scenesupervision.TheDepartmentappearstohavesufficientpoliciesand
proceduresinplacetoprovideforqualityNCUOFinvestigations.Non-compliancewasnot
causedsolelyatanyonelevelofreview(i.e.,investigatingsupervisor,watchcommander,Area
orBureau).Theproblemcontinuestobeinapplyingtherequirementswithconsistentand
unyieldingattentiontodetail.Deficientareasareresolvablethroughmanagement’semphasison
andcommitmenttothereformsmandatedbytheCD.TheDepartmentmustcontinuetoplace
emphasisonmanagementandsupervisoryattentiontodetail.

COMMANDACCOUNTABILITYPERFORMANCEAUDIT
77THSTREETAREAGANGENFORCEMENTDETAIL
Conductedby
AuditDivision
March2007
AuditDivisionpreviouslycompletedaCommandAccountabilityPerformanceAudit(CAPA)
for77thStreetAreaGangEnforcementDetail(GED)inFebruary2006.Findingsidentifiedin
thatauditwerewithintheEvaluationofWorkProduct,SupervisoryRolesandConduct,Gang
IntelligenceInformation,CriminalComplaintProcess,andCaseCategorizationobjectives.
Similartothefindingsoftheprevious77thStreetGEDCAPA,thisauditidentifiedfindingsin
thefollowingareas:
• EvaluationofWorkProduct,specifically,thelackofdocumentingWatchCommander
approval,date/timeandresultsofbookingsearches;
• EvaluationofSupervisoryRolesandConduct,specifically,lackoftimelycompletion,as
wellasoriginality,ofGEDpersonnel’sperformanceevaluations;
• EvaluationofGangIntelligenceInformation,specifically,theinputofdataintothe
CAL/GANGSystemwithintherequiredtimeandthemaintenanceofCAL/GANGCards;
• EvaluationoftheCriminalComplaintProcess,specifically,casesineligibleformodified
17(b)(4)PCprocessing;and,
• EvaluationofCaseCategorization,specifically,inappropriatelycategorizingcasesbasedon
specificguidelines,suchasinvestigativeleadsavailabletoidentifyandarrestthesuspect.
